### Document change in behavior of `Enum.group_by` ordering. (#4794)

**Author:** Myron Marston <myron.marston@gmail.com>
**Date:** 2016-06-09 11:40:11 -0700
**Commit:** a1bbc1bc207fb936de7c8241faf223b415fb1308

On 1.2, here's how `group_by` behaved:

    iex(1)> 1..10 |> Enum.group_by(fn i -> i < 5 end)
    %{false: [10, 9, 8, 7, 6, 5], true: [4, 3, 2, 1]}

On 1.3, here's how it behaves:

    iex(1)> 1..10 |> Enum.group_by(fn i -> i < 5 end)
    %{false: [5, 6, 7, 8, 9, 10], true: [1, 2, 3, 4]}

### Use heuristic for compiler errors (#4778)

**Author:** Gary Rennie <gazler@gmail.com>
**Date:** 2016-06-04 15:31:37 +0100
**Commit:** a0c0cb6358ba0577ed9f5f5f529e1868134a5893

Previously when all files being compiled get stuck waiting on
something else, we would release those files in random order.

This commit changes this to release files in groups based on
the module they are waiting on. We pick the module being
depended on with less edges, as it is mostly likely being the
cause of conflict. This may not always be true though: for example,
if there is a macro injecting code into multiple modules and such
code becomes faulty, now multiple modules are waiting on
this same module.

However, we have to pick one or the other, and we
decided to pick the one with fewer dependencies.

### Fix failing tests for System.cmd/3 on windows (#4760)

**Date:** 2016-06-03 01:54:06 +0200
**Commit:** ccf6d14e3ec2eb96090222dad6f395b5b9ab72ac

### ApplicationTest fails under Windows (#4761)

**Author:** robi-wan <robi-wan@users.noreply.github.com>
**Date:** 2016-06-03 01:53:26 +0200
**Commit:** e4e0f2d63346fb6c6e8cddc5e2310400f51bab5f

2) test application directory (ApplicationTest)
     test/elixir/application_test.exs:53
     Assertion with == failed
     code: String.downcase(Application.app_dir(:elixir)) == String.downcase(Path.join(root, "bin/../lib/elixir"))
     lhs:  "d:/home/docs/dev/git_repos/robi-wan-elixir/lib/elixir"
     rhs:  "d:/home/docs/dev/git_repos/robi-wan-elixir/bin/../lib/elixir"
     stacktrace:
       test/elixir/application_test.exs:55: (test)

`Application.app_dir(:elixir)` delivers under Windows (7 Pro 64bit) an absolute path:

iex(1)> Application.app_dir(:elixir)
"d:/home/docs/dev/git_repos/robi-wan-elixir/lib/elixir"

Under Linux (Ubuntu 16.04 64bit) it delivers a relative path:

iex(1)> Application.app_dir(:elixir)
"/home/robi-wan/development/work/robi-wan-elixir/bin/../lib/elixir"

`Application.app_dir/1` uses `:code.lib_dir` to retrieve the code path.
So maybe the different types of paths (relative vs absolute) are caused by Erlang.

Suggestion to fix the tests under Windows: Use Path.expand/1 to convert
the path (on the right hand side of the assertion) to an absolute path.

### Record.is_record/2: fixes bug when dealing with non-record tuples (#4697)

**Author:** eksperimental <eksperimental@users.noreply.github.com>
**Date:** 2016-05-27 12:58:19 -0500
**Commit:** ba6bbbf23e3252913917595fbf421373ff9e9ad8

First element in records must be an atom.
Before the fix, this is how it worked:

    iex> tuple = {"user", "john", 27}
    ...> Record.is_record(tuple)
    false
    ...> Record.is_record(tuple, "user")
    true

Record.is_record/1/2: add tests for when used in guard tests

### Clarify the use of `async: true` (#4692)

**Author:** Stephen Ball <sdball@gmail.com>
**Date:** 2016-05-27 02:51:48 -0400
**Commit:** aad62afb2e4844e817e2430af94eef5035b79949

The documentation here was a bit confusingly worded to make it sound
like with `async: true` each `test` declaration in the case would run
asynchronously. I've tried to reword it and make it clear that the flag
is set for the test case itself.

One example of the confusion is issue #3580. I found that thread when
trying to find the same answer for why six tests with
`:timer.sleep(1000)` were running in serial within a single test case.

### Keep brackets in the authority of URIs for IPv6 hosts

**Author:** Andrea Leopardi <an.leopardi@gmail.com>
**Date:** 2016-05-26 11:33:20 +0200
**Commit:** f3d938d0750c0376c7b54afa3b9aaa4e6b83fd78

Right now, when parsing a URI, we strip square brackets out of
hosts (meaning they're IPv6 hosts) and then use the stripped host to
rebuild the authority that will end up in the %URI{} struct. This means
that, for example:

    URI.parse("foo://[::]:1234").authority == ":::1234"

which is not good. In this commit, I fixed this by not rebuilding the
authority using the new host in URI.parse/1. The authority will now
include brackets around the host, but the :host field in the %URI{}
struct is still stripped.

----

### Add struct expansion via __struct__/1

**Author:** José Valim <jose.valim@plataformatec.com.br>
**Date:** 2016-05-26 12:40:12 +0200
**Commit:** dabe5e5102ee886230c85055ddf27e981cbd9139


----

### Use square brackets for IPv6 in URI.to_string/1

**Author:** Andrea Leopardi <an.leopardi@gmail.com>
**Date:** 2016-05-25 16:13:27 +0200
**Commit:** 0fa88d8d16beef927eb4d2030419ac785e72b4b9

According to the grammar at
https://tools.ietf.org/html/rfc3986#appendix-A, a host can have colons
in it only if it's a "IPv6" or a "IPvFuture"; in both cases, it's
expected to be surrounded by square brackets. This means that when
converting a URI to a string in URI.to_string/1, we can simply surround
a host with square brackets if it contains one or more colons.

### Disable overriding the async tag (#4649)

**Author:** João Britto <jabcalves@gmail.com>
**Date:** 2016-05-20 03:44:02 -0300
**Commit:** ab5e46ba625f7f62f99286a3fb48d010a292fa2c

The `async` config is meant to be set only by the time `ExUnit.Case`
is used. With these changes we make sure a proper error will be
raised if we eventually try to override it, either at the setup step,
for the whole case as a moduletag, or as a regular test tag.

### Fix failing doctest for Path.expand/1 under windows.

**Author:** robi-wan <robi-wan@suyu.de>
**Date:** 2016-05-19 00:28:09 +0200
**Commit:** 8997b632d3721f7e685b98be45f5e7e4ce946611

Test fails with this message:

     test doc at Path.expand/1 (11) (PathTest)
     test/elixir/path_test.exs:6
     Doctest failed
     code: Path.expand("/foo/bar/../bar") === "/foo/bar"
     lhs:  "d:/foo/bar"
     stacktrace:
       lib/path.ex:133: Path (module)

Remove iex> to disable this doctest.
This test is covered in path_test.exs.

----

### Fix failing test "expand path with user home" for Path.expand under Windows.

**Author:** robi-wan <robi-wan@suyu.de>
**Date:** 2016-05-19 00:04:45 +0200
**Commit:** 43a91beb1707a15dc65886fc5c4d110dad578acf

Test fails with this message:

     test expand path with user home (PathTest)
     test/elixir/path_test.exs:113
     Assertion with == failed
     code: home == Path.expand("~")
     lhs:  "C:\\Users\\username"
     rhs:  "c:/Users/username"
     stacktrace:
       test/elixir/path_test.exs:118: (test)

'home' is the value of 'System.user_home!' which uses
':elixir_config.get(:home)' which gets this value set from the call to
'init:get_argument(home)'.

Erlang delivers the user home with back slashes.
Path.expand/1 uses Path.absname/2 which converts the back slashes into slashes.

Simple fix: Compute 'home' using Path.absname/1

----

### Fix failing test "relative win" for Path.relative_to/2 under windows

**Author:** robi-wan <robi-wan@suyu.de>
**Date:** 2016-05-18 23:42:20 +0200
**Commit:** 8c836eb78bb7f40e0483be8d25e688d178e62e8b

Test fails with this message:

     test relative win (PathTest)
     test/elixir/path_test.exs:36
     Assertion with == failed
     code: Path.relative_to("D:/usr/local/foo", "d:") == "usr/local/foo"
     lhs:  "D:/usr/local/foo"
     rhs:  "usr/local/foo"
     stacktrace:
       test/elixir/path_test.exs:48: (test)

Path.relative_to/2 uses :filename.split/1 - split returns a drive letter with a trailing slash
(http://erlang.org/doc/man/filename.html#split-1)

iex(1)> Path.split("D:/usr/local/foo")
["d:/", "usr", "local", "foo"]
iex(2)> Path.split("D:")
["d:"]
iex(3)> Path.split("D:/")
["d:/"]

So the second argument needs a trailing slash to strip the drive information from given path.

### Windows: batch file improvements (#4642)

**Author:** robi-wan <robi-wan@users.noreply.github.com>
**Date:** 2016-05-18 22:22:41 +0200
**Commit:** 501f856a23d67de69adb106718bcd6b9a942df7d

* Add usage information to iex.bat

Support command line switch '--help', '-h', '/h' and '/?' to show usage information for iex.bat.
Check argument with '/I' to make the test case insensitive:
This allows showing usage information with '--HELP', '-H' and '/H'.

Use 'setlocal' and 'endlocal' so changes to the environment are local to the batch file
(https://technet.microsoft.com/en-us/library/bb491001.aspx) - so there is no need to un-set __ELIXIR_IEX_FLAGS.

* Use documented syntax 'goto label' - without leading colon before the label

* Support cli switch '/?' to show help

Use label :end to jump to the end of the batch file after showing help text -
so the call to elixir.bat is not executed when showing help.

Use 'setlocal' and 'endlocal' so that the env var 'argc' is local to this batch file.

* Support cli switch '/?' to show help for elixir.bat

Test for cli switch '/?' does not need to be case insensitive.

### Always add an environment and print it alongside the dep

**Author:** José Valim <jose.valim@plataformatec.com.br>
**Date:** 2016-05-18 11:27:03 +0200
**Commit:** eced1303efd2622f5080949290bdd9dc9b92c893

There are two fixes here:

* All dependencies run in a particular environment, which defaults
  to prod. If two dependencies have different environments, they
  will diverge but the error message wouldn't include the env flag,
  which made it hard to spot the reason. This commit now always
  include the :env option

* Our converger algorithm was comparing [env: :prod] with [] and
  accusing a divergence but they should be the same as the default
  :env is :prod. We have made sure to set the environment to :prod
  for all deps

### Friendly error message for duplicate ExUnit test names (#4613)

**Author:** Jay Hayes <jay@bignerdranch.com>
**Date:** 2016-05-13 09:17:54 -0500
**Commit:** e3443b9919c0223bc0ef47dfecbd3fcdd1ba69fc

Previously, duplicate test names would output a warning like:

  warning: this clause cannot match because a previous clause at line 6 always matches

In order to understand the warning, the user had know that ExUnit defines
functions based on the test names. This was an implementation detail that the
user shouldn't have to know about. Since the second test definition is useless,
make the definition of it fail immediately without running the tests and
explicitly tell the user why. The new error looks like:

  ** (ExUnit.DuplicateTestError) A test named "foo" is already defined in Elixir.DupTest.
      test/dup_test.exs:6: (module)
      (stdlib) erl_eval.erl:670: :erl_eval.do_apply/6

**Date:** 2016-04-29 16:17:32 +0200
**Commit:** 0f8838e9b1bed9ed7d423f28e93c116b843bbd05

Right now, the ../2 macro expands differently if the context is a
guard: it will expand to the AST representation of the %Range{} struct
with empty metadata. However, this metadata are not empty if what gets
unquoted is a variable or module attribute holding a range.

### IO: Several improvements (#4560)

**Author:** eksperimental <eksperimental@users.noreply.github.com>
**Date:** 2016-04-28 06:18:32 -0500
**Commit:** a70effd4b4367775f348b7d0c2e1a5c33ccc0dbd

IO Module:
  * Impoves wording in documentation, particularly in the use of :all, :line, count
  * Standardizes arg names to be `line_*`.
  * Adds function guards to check for integers.
  * Unicode is a proper noun, so it should be always capitalized.
  * Adds backticks to args suchs as `device`, `item`, `count`.
  * When functions are "Unicode unsafe", they are indicated in the first paragraph of docs.
  * IO.puts: code simplified removing unnecessary variable.
  * Adds \n to example in IO.gets/2
  * Adds example in IO.chardata_to_string/1 when dealing with strings
  * Renames args in IO.each_stream/2 and IO.each_binstream/2
  * Adds tests for IO.getn/2-3 calling functions with different arities

IO.Steam Module:
  * Adds a note about side effects when dealing with IO streams.

### Remove the use of the word `pad` from chunk

**Author:** José Valim <jose.valim@plataformatec.com.br>
**Date:** 2016-04-25 19:54:15 +0200
**Commit:** f266c5d5331b4091d65e88d267562c50226bb56b

We will now take the word `pad` to mean something used for
completion and cycled until the number of desired elements
are given.

Given this is not the behaviour in `chunk`, we renamed the
argument from `pad` to `leftover`.

This patch does not change the behaviour, it just amends
the documentation to use proper names.

### fix(IEx.pry/1): remove unreachable code (#4525)

**Author:** Utkarsh Kukreti <utkarshkukreti@gmail.com>
**Date:** 2016-04-17 13:35:41 +0530
**Commit:** 7e5ec5ddaaf4a6aa5e9e7be5e741fe5fecf64644

It seems like the "IEx cannot pry the shell itself" limitation was
removed 3 years ago in 0b624fff3964853a73e7f9c3c704eff8ecf613bc but this
code wasn't removed.

Found this thanks to Dialyzer and https://github.com/fishcakez/dialyze.

The error printed by Dialyzer was:

> lib/iex.ex:443: The pattern {'error', 'self'} can never match the type 'ok' | {'error','no_iex' | 'refused'}

### Integrate non-fetchable (path) dependencies into compilers

**Author:** José Valim <jose.valim@plataformatec.com.br>
**Date:** 2016-04-13 16:25:57 +0200
**Commit:** 6156b5b01cf466b820e983ba53e5758ab16ce623

This commit changes Elixir compilers so a path dependencies
no longer forces the "parent" project to recompile. This
means each compiler must know how to track dependencies but
it gives faster compilation times as a benefit.

### Only recompile empty files if they changed

**Author:** José Valim <jose.valim@plataformatec.com.br>
**Date:** 2016-04-13 12:53:34 +0200
**Commit:** e3a04eca10e2c690512552e90a23ce88d0064742

Prior to this commit, an Elixir file that did not
generate any modules would always be regenerated
whenever mix compile was invoked.

This commit addresses this issue by keeping source
files on its own rows in the Elixir manifest.

**Date:** 2016-04-07 05:02:14 +0700
**Commit:** 6a9e872a085b91374f8953e9fe7cbb071b37755f

* Latest changes introduced in Enum.sum changed the type of the return value
(from integer to float) when argument was a range. This commit fixes this.

* Adds a spec when dealing with Range.t

* It simplifies the function by making it more recursive,
and also deals with ranges where first and last are equal

### Handle chars literals in the parser for better syntax errors

**Author:** José Valim <jose.valim@gmail.com>
**Date:** 2016-04-04 21:35:12 +0200
**Commit:** 51feaa5e621b8c17eb0a06da16f45756b6fdb566

Handle chars literals in the parser for better syntax errors
----

### Handle chars literals in the parser for better syntax errors

**Author:** Andrea Leopardi <an.leopardi@gmail.com>
**Date:** 2016-03-28 17:58:23 +0200
**Commit:** 8619d26c4d8001ea52d9382ced0ddd1af4a80add

Before this commit, we handled char literals (like `?a`) in the
tokenizer, turning a literal like `?a` into the token `{:number, _,
97}` (thus indistinguishable from the literal `97` at the parsing
stage). This led to error messages with the integer for the character
instead of the character literal, e.g.:

    iex> :ok ?a
    ** (SyntaxError) iex:11: syntax error before: 97

With this commit, we now turn `?a` into the token `{:char, _,
97}` (which is the same token used by Erlang for Erlang char literals
like `$a`); since it's the same token as in Erlang, the parser will now
output the char literal as an Erlang char (`?a` would be printed as
`$a`). We hijack the error message in elixir_errors.erl to end up with
the correct message:

    iex> :ok ?a
    ** (SyntaxError) iex:11: syntax error before: ?a

**Author:** José Valim <jose.valim@gmail.com>

### Improve merge exception texts inside exunit callbacks

**Author:** azhi <astzhe@gmail.com>
**Date:** 2016-03-30 02:14:01 +0300
**Commit:** 410e2299deacb391202797122b794214ca9f4626

Considering this case:

```elixir
defmodule CallbacksCase do
  use ExUnit.Case

  setup do
    {:ok, :something}
  end

  test "nothing", do: assert true
end
```

Before this commit exunit callbacks return value merger was failing with

`(RuntimeError) expected ExUnit callback in CallbacksCase to return :ok or
{:ok, keyword | map}, got :something instead`.

But callback didn't return `:something`, it returned `{:ok, something}`.

After this commit same case will fail with

`(RuntimeError) expected ExUnit callback in CallbacksCase to return :ok or
{:ok, keywords | map}, got {:ok, :something} instead`.

This behaviour is achieved by moving all the checks to `__merge__/3` function.
`context_merge/3` now only works with maps and raises 'reserved keys' errors.

### Merge pull request #4450 from whatyouhide/anonymous-functions-in-pipes

**Author:** José Valim <jose.valim@gmail.com>
**Date:** 2016-03-30 13:53:42 +0200
**Commit:** 8e8336b75f30b48ccb972d50709eabb72ef33ac4

Handle non-called anonymous functions in Macro.pipe/3
----

### Handle non-called anonymous functions in Macro.pipe/3

**Author:** Andrea Leopardi <an.leopardi@gmail.com>
**Date:** 2016-03-30 11:22:17 +0200
**Commit:** 2faab1db7db59cfc2216be0fba9eab1c8951c677

Right now, this nasty error pops out:

    iex> :foo |> (fn x -> x end)
    ** (CompileError) iex:1: expected -> clauses in fn

This happens because of what we do in `Macro.pipe/3`, which is: we take
the AST of a function call as the second argument and insert the first
argument in the list of arguments for the function call, at the position
specified by the third argument. The AST for a `fn` looks exactly like
the AST of a function call (to the `:fn` function):

    iex> quote do: fn :ok -> :ok; :err -> :err end
    {:fn, [], [{:->, [], [[:ok], :ok]}, {:->, [], [[:error], :error]}]}

It's easy to see that the "arguments" to `:fn` are the `->` clauses in
the function. This means that doing `:foo |> (fn x -> x end)` basically
adds another clause to the function:

    iex> Macro.pipe(:foo, quote(do: fn :ok -> :ok; :err -> :err end), 0)
    {:fn, [], [:foo, {:->, [], [[:ok], :ok]}, {:->, [], [[:err], :err]}]}

and this makes `:elixir_fn.expand/3` blow up because it doesn't know how
to handle the `:foo` clause (because it's not a clause!).

With this commit, we blow up early in `Macro.pipe/3` when we see a
`{:fn, _, _}` call:

    iex> :foo |> (fn x -> x end)
    ** (ArgumentError) cannot pipe :foo into an anonymous function
    without calling the function; use :foo |> (fn x -> x end).()

### Add some comments to the Logger module

**Author:** Andrea Leopardi <an.leopardi@gmail.com>
**Date:** 2016-03-29 23:16:43 +0200
**Commit:** 7c5fa9a3b728d6ba09de1938cdcca149f004deee

I described in more depth what happens when calls to Logger macro are

----

### Suppress unused var warnings when removing Logger macros from the AST

**Author:** Andrea Leopardi <an.leopardi@gmail.com>
**Date:** 2016-03-27 11:32:07 +0200
**Commit:** a2885e26108584e328f38a5ed2d743229afa545e

Right now, code like this:

    defmodule MyMod do
      require Logger
      def my_fun(arg) do
        Logger.debug(arg)
        :ok
      end
    end

will generate an unused variable warning for the `arg` variable in
`my_fun/1` if the log level is higher than :debug. This happens because
in such cases, we completely remove the AST of the Logger call, so the
`arg` variable is effectively never used. This may seem magical to the
user.

With this commit, a Logger call which would have been removed from the
AST before now generates a series of `_ = x` expressions, one for each
variable passed to the Logger macro.

### Merge pull request #4444 from whatyouhide/aliases-in-pipes

**Author:** José Valim <jose.valim@gmail.com>
**Date:** 2016-03-29 20:16:47 +0200
**Commit:** 8594b7115c0784c199833b5b98479af411498bb8

Error out when passing :__aliases__ to Macro.pipe/3
----

### Error out when passing :__aliases__ to Macro.pipe/3

**Author:** Andrea Leopardi <an.leopardi@gmail.com>
**Date:** 2016-03-29 19:47:54 +0200
**Commit:** 7e77c913203ec61b09de139a9dedd61d125c54a0

Right now, this funny thing happens:

    iex> Macro |> Env
    Macro.Env

This happens because `|>/2` calls `Macro.pipe/3` under the hood, and
`Macro.pipe/3` inserts the left-hand side at the beginning of the list
of arguments in the AST for the function call on the right-hand
side. Aliases, however, look exactly like a call to the __aliases__
function:

    iex> quote do: Macro.Env
    {:__aliases__, [alias: false], [:Macro, :Env]}

and that's why `Macro |> Env` becomes `Macro.Env`. With this commit, we
explicitly take care of `:__aliases__` in a `Macro.pipe/3` call, so that
we can error out in such cases:

    iex> Macro |> Env
    ** (ArgumentError) cannot pipe Macro into Env, ...

Allow dots in the metadata info in Version.parse/1
----

### Allow dots in the metadata info in Version.parse/1

**Author:** Andrea Leopardi <an.leopardi@gmail.com>
**Date:** 2016-03-19 10:33:00 +0100
**Commit:** 6c3db5fd2c5080870089ca7df96a45518f4f4ea7

According to the SEMVER spec (http://semver.org/#spec-item-10):

  > Build metadata MAY be denoted by appending a plus sign and a series
  > of dot separated identifiers [...].

The dot was not previously allowed, with this commit it is.

  * `--logger-otp true|false` - to configure the `:handle_otp_reports`

  * `--logger-sasl true|false` - to configure the `:handle_sasl_reports`

### Merge pull request #4350 from whatyouhide/refactor-integer-parse

**Author:** José Valim <jose.valim@gmail.com>
**Date:** 2016-03-02 19:52:13 +0100
**Commit:** 7a82caea342cba2e5a2b36e8eef815d10f205d0c

Fix a possible binary leak in Integer.parse/1
----

### Fix a possible binary leak in Integer.parse/1

**Author:** Andrea Leopardi <an.leopardi@gmail.com>
**Date:** 2016-03-02 09:35:25 +0100
**Commit:** 45d5d32e30bfe5333c3c38bedeed6419e81a5cb1

Integer.parse/1 followed this pattern:

    defp do_parse(<<char, rest :: binary>>, ...) do
      ...
      # instead of re-using the original binary, we do this
      {..., <<char, rest :: binary>>}
    end

This causes a new binary to be created instead of `rest` just being a
sub-binary.

### Merge pull request #4340 from wojtekmach/import_warnings

**Author:** José Valim <jose.valim@gmail.com>
**Date:** 2016-02-28 20:31:07 +0100
**Commit:** 17368eb956637adc6c5a7f4df2cc2ac69a74f390

Track {module, function, arity} imports and warn on unused ones
----

### Track {module, function, arity} imports and warn on unused ones

**Author:** Wojtek Mach <wojtek@wojtekmach.pl>
**Date:** 2016-02-27 20:20:41 +0100
**Commit:** 743e8c7ed12ea71062d8b51164ec00ffcd92ecf5

Example:

Say, there's following file:

    # example.exs
    import String, only: [upcase: 1, downcase: 1]
    import Enum

    IO.puts upcase("Hello")

Before this patch:

    # $ elixir example.exs
    # HELLO
    # warning.exs:2: warning: unused import Enum

After this patch:

    # $ elixir example.exs
    # HELLO
    # example.exs:1: warning: unused import String.downcase/1
    # example.exs:2: warning: unused import Enum

[WIP] Correctly checkout rev when updating mix.exs
----

### Refer to the revision in mix.exs when updating deps

**Author:** Andrea Leopardi <an.leopardi@gmail.com>
**Date:** 2016-02-24 19:05:08 +0100
**Commit:** c77a1b2f62e13c01d60bc6322aaf464fe44dd8a4

Before this commit, we were correctly flagging a lock file as outdated
when the rev (branch/tag/rev) had been updated in mix.exs; at checkout
time, though, we were still looking at the lock file before looking at
mix.exs. Now, when checking out we look at mix.exs first and then fall
back to the lock file.

This should close #4309.

**Author:** José Valim <jose.valim@plataformatec.com.br>

### Reject non fullfilled optional dependencies later on

**Author:** José Valim <jose.valim@plataformatec.com.br>
**Date:** 2016-02-17 00:05:59 +0100
**Commit:** 68d5d9c07bf27889c71272dbd6fe350977ce110b

Imagine this dependency tree:

    * parent
      * child
        * ecto
        * postgrex
      * ja_serializer
        * ecto

where postgrex is an optional dependency of Ecto.

Because optional dependencies were rejected later on,
ecto would have its postgrex dependency stripped and
later failed to top-sort. This commit changes it to
so optional dependencies are rejected only later on.

Support :base option when inspecting binaries
----

### Support :base option when inspecting binaries

**Author:** Dmitry Kakurin <dmitry.kakurin@gmail.com>
**Date:** 2016-02-13 23:03:30 -0800
**Commit:** 5e272b8c10088d57a9090a80ed1a28287d3e80fd

Allow inspecting binaries as :hex or :octal.

Examples:

# as before
iex> inspect("abc")
"\"abc\""

# as before
iex> inspect("abc", binaries: :as_binary)
"<<97, 98, 99>>"

# NEW: base: :hex is recognized
iex> inspect("abc", binaries: :as_binary, base: :hex)
"<<0x61, 0x62, 0x63>>"

# NEW: any base other than :decimal implies binaries: :as_binaries
iex> inspect("abc", base: :hex)
"<<0x61, 0x62, 0x63>>"
iex> inspect("abc", base: :octal)
"<<0o141, 0o142, 0o143>>"

# NOTE: size is still represented as decimal
iex> inspect(<< 10, 11, 12::4 >>, base: :hex)
"<<0xA, 0xB, 0xC::size(4)>>"

### Improvements to the parallel compiler

**Author:** José Valim <jose.valim@plataformatec.com.br>
**Date:** 2016-02-13 13:33:16 +0100
**Commit:** b6dc389f5e3eb2286885c172009333ef414d7860

In previous versions, when trying to compile a file and the file
depended on a module that was not yet compiled or was unavailable,
the parallel compiler would wait until the missing dependency shows
up. In case it did not, the compiler would "release" the file being
compiled, disabling any further missing dependency resolution.

This lead to issues in code like the following:

    Code.ensure_compiled(SomeModuleThatWontBeAvailable)
    %SomeStructFromMyProject{}

Because `SomeModuleThatWontBeAvailable` would never be available,
the file above would block until released. When released, the
parallel compiler would disable further lookups for that file,
meaning we would no longer wait for missing dependencies when we
attempt to load the struct.

In the majorify of cases, the struct would already be available,
which meant compilation would work. But if the file that defined
the struct was also blocked by the compiler (and consequently
released), compilation would be non-deterministic.

This patch solves the issue by removing the "release" behaviour
from the compiler. If a dependency is not available, we will
notify the file of such and move on, with the parallel compiler
still enabled. In order to guarantee this works without
introducing deadlocks, the parallel compiler now also knows which
module is being defined by a given file, building a graph and
solving dependencies from the leaves. In case it is a cyclic
graph, we will raise that a deadlock was found.

Closes #4294
Closes #4055

----

### Make GenServer.cast/2 always return :ok. Closes #4251

**Author:** Martin Kavalar <martin@sauspiel.de>
**Date:** 2016-02-03 09:59:22 +0100
**Commit:** d6415a59296bafae0dad88999dcd9251e91d1385

This unifies the behavior of `GenServer.cast/2` to always
return :ok and never raise an `ArgumentError`, regardless
of how it is called.

Previously an `ArgumentError` would be be raised only when
called with a nonexistant local process name. In all other cases
which are calling using `:global`, `:via` or `{name, node}`
it would previously always return `:ok`.

The new behavior is now constistant with `:gen_server.cast/2`.

This reverts the change partially introduced in 0d4af57abead1c4dfdc2f71fe198ae539f6eceab.

See #4251 for the full discussion.

Add test attributes
----

### Add test attributes

**Author:** Brian Cardarella <brian@dockyard.com>
**Date:** 2016-01-27 19:56:46 -0500
**Commit:** c4abdb1d637eae9871379ebf6759cc55fc9f2314

ExUnit.Case can declare an optional `@test_attributes` which are
accumlated per test run and injected into the `context` under the
attribute name nested under `registered`:

```elixir
defmodule MyTest do
  use ExUnit.Case
  @test_attributes :foo

  @foo hello: "world"
  test "can access @foo via `context`", context do
    assert context.registered.foo.hello == "world"
  end
end
```

**Author:** Aleksei Magusev <lexmag@me.com>

**Commit:** aa328c7e7eb03f9a2de495edcd8bfcb8d18420a9

Stream.resource/3 @doc and @spec change
----

### Support passing io_device to File.copy/3

**Author:** Parker Selbert <parker@sorentwo.com>
**Date:** 2016-01-21 16:39:33 -0600
**Commit:** 80c7c0b36756349b0c0fcffca209e416f6cc078c

Technically it is possible to pass an io_device to File.copy/3 as either
the source, destination, or both. However, all input was being converted
to a string, which fails for any non-binary data and prevents
`:file.copy` from being reached.

This updates the typespec and conditionally converts chardata to a
string. The file error message now inspects both source and destination
for uniform logging.

----

### Stream.resource/3 @doc and @spec change

**Author:** Justin <calleja.justin@gmail.com>
**Date:** 2016-01-23 15:23:23 +0100
**Commit:** 0f3f284e84af2594658384ce974a858a1da7073e

@doc change: there is no Stream.transform/2 and Stream.transform/4's
initial accumulated value is also computed lazily so the doc in
Stream.resource/3 must be referring to Stream.transform/3.

@spec change: I tried the example in Stream.resource/3 to use `nil`
instead of `{:halt, acc}`, as currently specified by the spec, and got:

** (CaseClauseError) no case clause matching: nil
    (elixir) lib/stream.ex:1099: Stream.do_resource/5
    (elixir) lib/enum.ex:1486: Enum.reduce/3
    (elixir) lib/enum.ex:2248: Enum.to_list/1

Besides, the doc and spec don't match (and the doc seems to be correct,
so I changed the spec).

### Do not warn in match assertion if variable is reused in pattern

**Author:** Aleksei Magusev <lexmag@me.com>
**Date:** 2016-01-06 19:58:21 +0100
**Commit:** e1904749557f6cf62f6638d3901930d69a2896c4

Having the folowing assertion `assert {var, var} = {1, 1}`,
the code produced previously:

  [var] = case(right) do
    {var, var} ->
      #...
      [var]
    _ ->
      #...
  end

And with the patch:

  [var, var] = case(right) do
    {var, var} ->
      #...
      [var, var]
    _ ->
      #...
  end

### Merge pull request #4169 from sorentwo/note-atoms-required

**Author:** José Valim <jose.valim@gmail.com>
**Date:** 2016-01-06 19:13:49 +0100
**Commit:** 2fb2e9a97fc02cff9318b559fe5ba150545b639e

Document that non-atom keys are ignored by struct
----

### Document that non-atom keys are ignored by struct

**Author:** Parker Selbert <parker@sorentwo.com>
**Date:** 2016-01-06 11:42:56 -0600
**Commit:** 2f41033314e69c502c3e2abd75d9423ca6d29217

Maps coming from decoded JSON or other untrusted sources usually have
string keys. Attempting to convert a map with string keys into a struct
will always result in the default struct, as structs are always defined
with atoms so the keys are all ignored.

feat(Kernel): raise when defstruct is called more than once
----

### feat(Kernel): raise when defstruct is called more than once

**Author:** Gary Rennie <gazler@gmail.com>
**Date:** 2016-01-04 18:46:15 +0000
**Commit:** d7e295d11d06df13904a57b4856b757e473af937

Previously `defstruct` could be called multiple times. This could be
particularly confusing is `defstruct` is called by an external library
such as the Ecto `schema` macro.

Calling `defstruct` when the `@struct` module attribute already exists
will now cause an `ArgumentError`

This closes #4155

### Improve error message for when the named mix task cannot be found.

**Author:** Myron Marston <myron.marston@gmail.com>
**Date:** 2016-01-02 20:31:28 -0800
**Commit:** 08748dc364aabd14091519bbfac3eecb54d656a1

Previously, you could get the incredibly confusing error:

> The task "acronym.http" could not be found. Did you mean "acronym.http"?

This happened when you named your module `Mix.Tasks.Acronym.HTTP` rather
than `Mix.Tasks.Acronym.Http` as expected. Since we can detect this
situation, it's helpful to give the user a clear explanation of the
problem.
----

### abbreviations -> acronyms

**Author:** Myron Marston <myron.marston@gmail.com>
**Date:** 2016-01-02 20:41:34 -0800
**Commit:** 37a00dfb38a247b32ab2a79f1d5408a243b5a139

Using all capitals is recommended only when you have an acronym
like HTTP, IO, SCM, etc. For an abbreviation like "Misc" or "Utils"
all caps should not be used.

### Optional else clauses can be added to special form with

**Author:** Dmitry Russ <dmitry.a.russ@gmail.com>
**Date:** 2015-12-25 11:28:55 +0100
**Commit:** 0aac5a71226b7f3bbd29e2135a99c9e85593d5e3

If else conditions are given, then wrapping case will be generated
around with case clauses. In a case, if else conditions doesn't have
a match all clause, than it will be added clause, which raise
WithClauseError.

Reference: #4085

### Override some opts passed to yecc in compile.yecc

**Author:** Andrea Leopardi <an.leopardi@gmail.com>
**Date:** 2015-12-24 19:36:17 +0100
**Commit:** 77afcf9d9748449c47f6d756b736d6eacdae3b82

Some options change the return value of `:yecc.file/2` and that can make
a `case` statement (in `Mix.Compilers.Erlang.interpret_result/2`)
fail. This commit overrides those "dangerous" options.
